--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (G and I) in the given graph, we need to analyze all possible paths between them and identify the necessary nodes to sever all connections.

### Step-by-Step Reasoning:

1. **Identify the Black Nodes:**
   - The black nodes are G and I.

2. **Trace All Paths Between G and I:**
   - Directly connected to G: H, B, E
   - Directly connected to I: H, P, C, Y
   - Paths from G to I:
     - G -> H -> I
     - G -> B -> C -> I
     - G -> B -> Y -> I
     - G -> E -> M -> F -> X -> K -> J -> P -> I

3. **Identify Critical Nodes:**
   - Node H is directly connected to both G and I.
   - Node B connects G to multiple nodes that can lead to I.
   - Node E connects G to multiple nodes that can lead to I.
   - Node P is directly connected to I and can be reached from G through multiple paths.

4. **Determine the Minimum Cut Set:**
   - Removing H will sever the direct path G -> H -> I.
   - Removing B will sever the paths G -> B -> C -> I and G -> B -> Y -> I.
   - Removing E will sever the path G -> E -> M -> F -> X -> K -> J -> P -> I.

   By removing H and B, we effectively sever all paths between G and I because:
   - G -> H -> I is severed by removing H.
   - G -> B -> C -> I and G -> B -> Y -> I are severed by removing B.
   - The path G -> E -> M -> F -> X -> K -> J -> P -> I still requires either H or B to be intact for any connection to be made.

### Final Answer:
Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [H, B].

   - Removing E and I is the minimal cut set.

   - Removing node O will cut off multiple paths leading from J towards L.
   - Removing node H will also cut off multiple paths leading from J towards L.

3. **Determine Minimum Cut Set:**
   - To ensure no paths exist between J and L, we need to find the minimum set of nodes that, when removed, will definitely disconnect these paths.

   - Removing node **D** blocks all possible starting routes from J.

   - Removing node **E** ensures that all access to L from any remaining nodes is cut off.

4. **Evaluate Each Cut Set:**

   - Removing O and E immediately ensures no paths can extend from J to L.
   - Removing either D or H doesn't suffice if the other is retained or given an alternate path to connect through different intersections.
